#2137. Tare discusses new Milan direction, limitations in his role and ‘jealousy’: “Better late than never”

Igli Tare has spoken without holding anything back about his time at AC Milan, explaining his frustration with several things.

When Tare arrived, many Milan fans were happy to see someone with Serie A experience oversee the project and whilst his first season didn’t end how anyone had hoped, he wasn’t seen as a major issue.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

So, when it was announced that he was part of a complete overturning of the management, there was a bit of a surprise, even if it did feel inevitable at one point.

With the overhaul of changes in the summer, one does wonder what Tare would have done differently, especially with suggestions that his hands were tied in the mercato last season. Nevertheless,

Speaking on Sky Sport’s ‘Calcio Unplugged’, Igli Tare revealed all to Gianluca Di Marzio and Stefano Borghi in an in-depth interview about his time with the Diavolo, as Radio Rossonera has relayed.

“Today is the first time I’ve spoken out after all these months. The wound of not having reached the Champions League is still enormous, and since I arrived at Milan – also questioning myself with the type of contract I signed – the objective couldn’t be only the Champions League; otherwise I wouldn’t have understood where I was.”

“It was great because he was the one who convinced me. He had such a huge desire to join Milan. I wanted to video chat before going to see him because I couldn’t believe it. But after five minutes, I knew in my head that he was the player Milan really lacked.

“Not just because of his qualities, but as a man and as a figure that that team didn’t have. He never said no. A photo with a child, an autograph, a meeting, talking to someone… He never said no. These are the things that humility is key to in life.

“I’m proud to have brought a great champion to Milan. In my opinion, if he wants, he can play another two seasons without any problem. He never missed a training session. Sometimes we were the ones to stop him because he was always there, ready. Loving football at that age is truly an honour.”

Is it a characteristic of the champion or is it a generational issue? Today’s champions seem to have a different mentality:

“Today we live in the age of social media. At 16, you’re already a footballer who can earn millions of euros. Living this way, you have the problem of not only managing a player, who is still a child, but you have to manage the business they bring with them: family, agents, girlfriends…

“These are obstacles that didn’t exist back then. We were a generation that played in the streets. Back then, our parents beat us to get us to come home. Today, however, it’s to get them out of the house because they spend all day in front of computers.

“This aspect has been lost a bit, but it’s our fault. Education and discipline are the key to success.”

Fullkrug. He performed poorly in Milan, but in the Bundesliga he’s scoring a lot and is the top scorer. How do you explain that?

“It’s simple. He came to us after a tough time in England, with a lot of scepticism. And then, right away, he broke his toe. No one ever said he’d been having injections on the bone for two months just to train, let alone to play.

“I knew he was a centre forward that the team was missing; given his characteristics and his interpretation of the game, his qualities were unquestionable. Just two years before coming to Milan, he’d played in the Champions League final, playing a key role in a competition with Borussia Dortmund.

“There are situations where, for a player, even a very important one, it matters how you feel. If you feel appreciated, valued, taken into consideration… When you play for a club like Milan, the weight of the shirt is completely different than when you play for other clubs.

“You feel it from the first day you join. I had no doubt that he could be what he’s demonstrating now.”

“We tried to sign him back in June. We made a first attempt. In the final days of the transfer window, another important name we tried to sign was Akanji. The Champions League was crucial for both of them. They turned him down, not because of the club’s importance, but because he wasn’t playing in the Champions League.

“Then the opportunity arose again at the end of August for Rabiot. Defeat against Cremonese? No, we had already decided to bring him. It was just a matter of contract details.”

“It was difficult for us because we decided to change the formation five days before the transfer window closed. Switching from a 4-3-3 to a three-man defense meant we were having a really hard time finding a quality player in the last five days.

“We were a bit unlucky, first with the Akanji story and then with Liverpool’s Joe Gomez… Guehi’s move from Crystal Palace to Liverpool fell through, and at the last minute they—we already had the deal ready, we were just waiting for this to happen—made a step back. And to avoid being left without a central defender at the last minute, we signed Odogu as a promising player.”

On not being able to reach the Champions League and not being able to continue his work at Milan…

“This is the first time today, after all these months, that I’ve spoken about this… The wound of not reaching the Champions League is still enormous. I think that from the first day I arrived at Milan, even in the way I signed—I questioned myself with the type of contract.

“I said, ‘If I have to come only for the Champions League objective, then I don’t understand where I’ve come from.’ Milan has a different story. I had to go through a trial. The disappointment is that those who run the club didn’t understand how much time people needed, which is now being done, rightly so.

“That is to say, it takes several transfer sessions to get a competitive team ready to win. At the beginning, I felt the responsibility to put the pieces together immediately, because after the defeat in the Coppa Italia final the year before, there were some important signs.

“I didn’t know where to start. But I think we did an important job last summer, we sold for a lot and also signed 10 new players. Then you need time to integrate, learn new philosophies and create a cohesive group, hungry to win.

“Until March-April, we were a group with difficulties, but we were at the top of the table. Then in the last 10 matchdays, we had a mental breakdown, also due to other external dynamics, which I don’t want to discuss here, which had a significant impact.

“Internal or external dynamics? External to the team. But we also have some blame: myself first and foremost, along with Max [Allegri].

“We had to be a little more attentive to certain dynamics. We needed to be more present with the team to ultimately lead them to the final result. Isolation is the wrong word; I don’t believe in these things. I believe in teamwork; I believe in cohesion: from the club to the team.

“When this happens, you transmit it to the team. When these situations become possible, you could also see it in the team’s results, because the players are very attentive. Sometimes we think they have other interests, but they pay attention to everything that comes out. They follow everything very carefully and transmit it.

“Instilling serenity… How can you convey serenity? When, in difficult moments, you show that you believe in the project more than before. Sometimes I missed this feeling, a lack of something that could give us the final push.”

But despite this, did you think you could continue? Does this farewell after only a year feel like a stab in the back?

“Look, I would have asked for a round table where we could all sit down together and analyze everything. Then they could have just said ‘no, thanks’… But I respect the decision they made. I accept it with great regret, but I’ve never questioned it because there’s someone above me who has to decide, including for the fate of the club.

“And when they decide to take a different path, you have to accept it. I know things could have gone differently, even much better. I’ll always be a Milan fan, even after an experience like that.”

Was AC Milan a team that needed the constant presence of a manager or coach to avoid letting itself go as it later did?

“The feeling I had at the beginning was that this leadership was a bit lacking. The arrival of Modric—and another of our goals was also Granit Xhaka, Leverkusen’s captain—was precisely to bring real players into the group, with important characteristics and capable of leading others.

“Granit didn’t arrive; Luka arrived, who has a different way of being a leader: he does it in silence and with his attitude on the pitch. I had the opportunity to meet Mike Maignan: an extraordinary guy, a soul who cares deeply about the AC Milan shirt.

“Gabbia is another extraordinary guy; he lives for this club. Rabiot too… His experience spoke for itself. The great disappointment is that I expected something more in terms of responsibility and leadership from Pulisic: he’s a top-class player with unquestionable qualities. But in terms of performance, things happened that I had never experienced in my career…

“Like Gimenez: an extraordinary guy with numbers that spoke for themselves; for a year he failed to score a goal. Then it happened that Pulisic didn’t score for six months. He scored a goal. Then Leao didn’t score for four months.

“Situations that are hard to come by in football have happened… It can happen that you don’t score for a month, two months, but that long a period of time also affected the team’s final performance.

“Losing a match against Cagliari the way we did, 2-1, I think was the worst match of my life. The feeling and the disappointment I felt towards the fans in the stadium that night is something I will never forget for the rest of my life.”

If Cardinale had been the Cardinale he is today, the one who is also physically present, would you still be AC ​​Milan’s DS?

“I can’t answer that question; he has to answer it. I can say that I remember when the Jashari negotiations were going on… I had just landed in Rome and I was very pleased when he called me because he wanted to understand what was happening in that negotiation.

“I explained all the dynamics to him and I really liked that he said, ‘Anything you need, if you have to spend a little more, let me know because I’m here.’ Then during that process I never had the chance to talk to him, even intentionally.

“There were other people above me who I never wanted to bypass. I never had a direct relationship with him, and the possibility of a discussion would have helped everyone. Not me, but everyone. By putting all the problems and dynamics on the table, we could have resolved things much sooner. But I think it’s a new experience for Cardinale.

“He’s had experience in other sports in America. Now he’s done something important, taking responsibility and putting his face on the line. I would have preferred to have had that presence even when I was there because it would have been very useful. But for the Milan, better late than never.

“Now, for better or worse, he will make his own decisions and responsibilities. I think that for him, too, the best interests of Milan come first.”

Was Leao a problem or a resource in your mind?

“I knew him from the outside. When I had the chance to meet him in person, I thought he was an extraordinary guy. I truly loved him as a person, like everyone else. But he was the player from whom we all expected something more. I think football was no longer a priority for him. That’s the key to understanding him.”

“I think a bit about his life outside of football. Music, fashion, things… But I never doubted the sense of belonging he had towards Milan. You could sense that he loved playing for Milan and was suffering from not performing.

“He had many physical problems… First a calf, then a bit of pubalgia… He always played with his hands under control. I always tried to have individual conversations with him more than with any other Milan player.

“Every time I spoke to him, I left with a positive feeling. I sensed that he wanted to. But sometimes wanting isn’t enough.”

Was there a chance that Leao would leave during your summer too?

“A concrete offer never came for him. Regarding the other transfers, it’s not just Tare’s job. When I arrived – even at Lazio – I was a bit alone, but I tried to involve all the members of the club to give him the importance he needed to build a sense of belonging and responsibility. I think I did a very good job, together with Furlani, Ibra, and Moncada.

“We achieved significant sales figures, in a year when we weren’t even in the Champions League. Thanks to the work we did last summer, investments were made this year, with the money we had at the time. I really believe in teamwork.

“I said it even before the Coppa Italia match against Bari; I asked to speak to convey the importance of the work of the people within the club because it was right that way. Then afterwards, it’s human…

“They were coming off a bad year, when two cumbersome figures like Tare and Allegri arrive, and everyone was talking about Tare and Allegri, some disappointment was understandable. I understood the feeling they had, but I could… There was little to do about it.

“For me, it was a new path, a new opportunity. It was essential to work as a team and present ourselves as a real company.”

Cardinale clearly delegated it all to Furlani and used Ibra for oversight. That is now confirmed. But Tare seems to let Cardinale off the hook a bit for this. Wrong, Cardinale is who didn’t provide the proper oversight either directly or with an actual adult in the room.

Let’s hope that was the last of Gerry’s big bungles

I’m not surprised. He’s always come off as a bit of a d*ck to me. Then there was the confirmation about Leao. More interesting is what he said, or didn’t say about Furlani:

“I explained all the dynamics to him and I really liked that he said, ‘Anything you need, if you have to spend a little more, let me know because I’m here.’ Then during that process I never had the chance to talk to him, even intentionally.

He’s talking about being blocked from talking to Cardinale by Furlani.

And you should praise him for that because we all knew (about) that but it is easy for us to post and comment, use all the words we could think of. Was it great, is he someone that we should lament over? Probably not, but, hey, I’d like (or maybe I wouldn’t, if you know what I mean) to see the others in such situations and how they would have handled them (or not)…

#2136. Bolton Wanderers should move for underused Middlesbrough man as they look to avoid Championship relegation battle: Opinion

Bolton Wanderers have made a positive start to life in the Championship since winning promotion in the summer. Steven Schumacher‘s Trotters sit seven points above the bottom three after 15 games; a good position, but they will know there is still a lot of work to do.

Despite their good start, BWFC have found a number of issues that they will have to fix if they want to remain in the Championship. One of those issues has been depth in midfield, with Schumacher’s squad containing just five players they deem ready to play in the central areas.

Attacking midfield has been a particularly unsettled area, with Atsuke Ito and Ruben Rodrigues failing to lock down the position. One solution would be to make a loan move for Middlesbrough’s Leo Castledine.

The 21-year-old has featured in just eight games for Boro, and with Kim Hellberg‘s side hunting promotion, it seems unlikely that he will force his way into the starting picture. It means the door is ajar for Bolton Wanderers to swoop in and pick up a player who could help them avoid relegation.

For Schumacher and co, it is all about staying in the Championship this season. Depth in all areas will be important to do just that, and Bolton Wanderers need it in the attacking midfield role.

The Trotters play a 4-2-3-1 formation that utilises a single attacking midfielder. Ito and Rodrigues have been the main two players to play the role, with three goals coming from the pair. The problem is, both have spent time on the injury table, and neither player has hit the heights Schumacher needs.

Castledine, who showed just what he can do at the position with Huddersfield Town in 2025/26, would slot in not only as welcome depth, but as a player who knows the role inside out.

Castledine’s move from Chelsea to Middlesbrough has not yet worked out and Boro have other priorities this season. He has not found a regular role and with promotion at the forefront of Hellberg’s mind, moving the 21-year-old on to strengthen his squad and help the player develop suits all.

That opens the door for Bolton Wanderers to pick up a quality player in an area of need for a low-cost deal. It satisfies all parties, including Castledine’s need for regular football and Middlesbrough’s desire to add quality as they push for the Premier League.

Other clubs are sure to be lurking, but BWFC could be a great destination for Castledine as he looks for a chance to prove his ability in the Championship.

#2134. "Stai zitto. Non sai niente", "il suo calcio è finito": tra Allegri e Adani la guerra continua

"Stai zitto, ora parlo io. Non sai niente di calcio". Chi si è dimenticato la virale sfuriata di Massimiliano Allegri contro l'allora opinionista di Sky Sport Daniele Adani alzi la mano. Difficile, forse impossibile, per i video che ciclicamente vengono riproposti sul web e uno scontro che ormai si protrae da quel 27 aprile 2019. Nel dopopartita di Inter-Juventus (1-1), in collegamento sull'emittente satellitare.

Il tecnico livornese punta il dito contro chi fa teoria calcistica, rivendicando gli Scudetti vinti e arrivando a dire ad Adani: "Te sei il primo che leggi libri e non sai niente di calcio. Sei lì dietro e non sai niente, perché fare l'allenatore è un altro discorso". Qui il confronto a distanza degenera allo "stai zitto" di Allegri, perché l'ex difensore replica immediatamente: “Stai zitto lo dici a tuo fratello". Il tecnico bianconero si sfila il microfono di Sky Sport e lascia il collegamento. Il giorno dopo la lite continua: Allegri riceve il Tapiro d’Oro e ironizza, definendo Adani "un bravo ragazzo". "Lo reputo un valido professionista. Sono andato via perché i miei amici mi aspettavano per cena". Adani non si è trattenuto, anzi ha parlato di un atteggiamento "scortese, arrogante e maleducato" da parte del tecnico toscano. Dopo l’eliminazione dalla Champions League contro l’Ajax, l'ex centrale nerazzurro aveva anche criticato la mancanza di un piano B della Juve. E Allegri, senza nominarlo, aveva risposto di stare "imparando tanto da quelli che spiegano il calcio" in uno show in conferenza stampa.

Il rapporto tra i due non è però fatto solo di scontri. Nel novembre 2025, dopo il derby vinto dal suo Milan contro l'Inter, Allegri riceve da Adani un riconoscimento in diretta alla Domenica Sportiva: "Onore al merito per quello che stai facendo", riguardo il suo operato manageriale sulla panchina rossonera. Dura poco e a fine stagione il Diavolo resta tagliato fuori dalla Champions, ritrovandosi in Europa League dopo un logorante cedimento del progetto e l'esonero del club meneghino. Il 26 ottobre 2026, dopo il 3-3 del suo Napoli con la Roma, Adani torna alla carica e ha dichiarato che "il calcio di Allegri è finito, basta con le pagliacciate".

Nel corso della trasmissione YouTube Viva el Futbol, in compagnia di Antonio Cassano e Nicola Ventola, Adani ha additato Allegri per la sistemazione tattica di Kevin De Bruyne al Napoli: "Ma perché si è voluto raccontare, come quando Steve Jobs ha inventato l'iPhone, che mettere davanti alla difesa De Bruyne era lo sbarco sulla Luna, cioè l'invenzione del secolo? A me non è dispiaciuto tra le linee, in quello che fa da vent'anni. Si è voluto far passare come una genialata". Un collegamento lampante con la Nazionale belga a far riflettere, dopo il 4-1 inferto all'Italia: Ma voi avete visto che fottuta partita ha fatto Kevin De Bruyne tra la linea di centrocampo e di difesa dell'Italia? Con che qualità, pulizia, anticipo? Un'arte calcistica tra le linee che imbarazza". E sulle critiche ricevute dal giocatore ha aggiunto: “Chi ha detto che De Bruyne è un problema per il Napoli dovrebbe imbarazzarsi, dovrebbe provare imbarazzo per il calcio, per De Bruyne innanzitutto, ma soprattutto per la sua storia”.

Due idee di calcio contrapposte, due poli opposti. Si può tradurre così la tensione evidente tra Max Allegri e Lele Adani nel corso degli anni, il racconto del gioco "vero" contro il pragmatismo del risultato. Idee e proposte contro chi mette al centro il raggiungimento del risultato, perché è l'unica cosa che conta, direbbe qualcuno. La "guerra" prosegue e sullo sfondo restano quelle etichette tanto consumate in Italia, "giochista" contro "risultatista". Ognuno finisce per interpretare il calcio a modo suo. Chi dalla panchina e chi, invece, nei panni di opinionista.

#2133. Lazio, Agostinelli sul caso di Baldacchino: "Speravo la situazione si potesse risolvere"

Il giudizio riguarda chi è rimasto a Roma durante la sosta; Agostinelli racconta anche di aver visto Frattesi sorridente.

La Lazio non ha staccato la spina durante la sosta: è questo l’aspetto che ha più colpito il protagonista dell’intervento riportato da Radiosei e che gli dà sicurezza per il futuro. A suo giudizio, i giocatori rimasti a Roma hanno mostrato di voler restare concentrati e di meritare spazio. Nel suo intervento ha parlato anche degli infortuni, di Frattesi e della sua conoscenza con Kurt Baldacchino.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

L’intervento si apre con una precisazione: «Premesso che la mia conoscenza con Kurt (Baldacchino, ndr) risale a tre anni fa.»

Lui è il presidente del ‘Lazio club Malta’ e appunto tre anni fa mi premiò quando allenavo l’altra squadra di Malta (il Gudja United Club). Quest’anno si divide tra la mia squadra, il Mġarr United, e le giovanili. Sapevo di questa cosa e pensavo si potesse risolvere. Quando siamo arrivati, io sono andato a casa mia a Roma e con la squadra e ci siamo rivisti direttamente a Formello, incontro Kurt, bravo ragazzo e tifosissimo della Lazio, e mi dice che era rimasto fuori. Eravamo però a ridosso della gara, quindi ho raggiunto la squadra. Speravo la situazione si potesse risolvere poi invece non è andata così e mi è molto dispiaciuto perché pensavo potesse essere un ‘giorno di festa’. Ad ogni modo è un ragazzo splendido, ognuno può esprimere le sue idee non gli darei ‘la croce addosso’. Probabilmente il fatto si poteva risolvere in maniera serena.

Gli infortuni della Lazio? Avendo degli staff molto numerosi ci sono confronti continui. Oggi lo staff è molto importante. Frattesi l’ho visto sorridente. Per me un giocatore può fare mille cose atletiche ma lo giudico ‘guarito’ se fa una partita, anche solo di 15′ perché non sei frenato durante la partitella. Questo però non significa che non ci saranno rischi in gara.

Di questa Lazio l’aspetto che mi ha impressionato, e che mi dà sicurezza per il futuro, è che non hanno staccato la spina. Questo non è importante, di più. Significa che chi era a Roma durante la sosta ha voluto dimostrare di stare sul pezzo, di meritare spazio”.

#2132. “Important to take time” – Gabbia admits January return is possible with right precautions

Matteo Gabbia has admitted that he could return to the field in January for AC Milan, but he doesn’t want to rush back.

It has been a difficult season for Milan, at least where injuries are concerned. In defence, they have had issues reminiscent of the 2023/24 season, where Gabbia had to be brought back from his loan spell at Villarreal.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

This season, though, it seems Gerry Cardinale is going to go down a different route and spend to help Ruben Amorim with his defensive options. Indeed, there have been plenty of rumours about recruitments at the back.

However, Gabbia could again be a key returnee in January, with him hoping to return from injury early in the new year.

Speaking to Sky Sport (via MilanNews) – at an event organised by the Milan Foundation at Milan’s “Arturo Toscanini” secondary school, where they met with several children with disabilities – Gabbia discussed the importance of the event, as well as his recovery.

“It’s wonderful, very enjoyable to spend hours with them. It’s also beautiful to see how they manage to create a group, something that really shows the importance of teamwork, the importance of friendship, even among themselves.

“We’re happy; we thank Fondazione Milan for the project, which is always beautiful and important: we’re happy to be a part of it and we’re enjoying the afternoon.”

Are you able to rejoin the group in January?

“Yes, that could be the time: we’re trying to work hard and do everything as best we can. I obviously have to thank the physiotherapists and the doctor because we’re really doing everything we can every day to try to heal the injury and get back on the pitch feeling good.

“It’s definitely been an unlucky season for me so far: I don’t want it to happen again or for any recurrences, so it’s also important to take the right time, get back in shape, and support the team right now.”

“Although it’s bad for the team that there are so many injured players, we’re together right now, supporting each other and trying to encourage each other through therapy, gym work, and the entire recovery process. Therefore, we’re trying to work as a team this week too, to overcome all the players’ injuries.

“From a personal standpoint, I’m trying to take this time calmly, in the sense that these things are part of sport, part of life, and not all moments are the same.

“This is certainly a difficult time, but it still allows me to stay close to the team, to try to lend my support, even in the good and bad times. That’s what I’m doing, in addition to obviously doing what I can to try to return as soon as possible and make my contribution on the pitch.”

How do you explain the team’s ups and downs?

“I think it’s something we need to get our heads around; we need to understand the importance of every game.

“The other day I heard our coach give an interview in which he said something important: we need to understand that every game, when you don’t win, is taken as a defeat, a heavy defeat. We need to truly understand the importance of every game and approach training to prepare 100%, as best as possible, for every match.

“Therefore, I think this is room for improvement, but I see a group that is really eager to improve, to work, and as a result, I’m sure we’ll be able to fix this flaw as well.

“Although we’ve had a good start to the year in the league and less so in the Europa League, we have plenty of time to improve and have a strong year.”

#2130. West Ham keeping tabs on upcoming Bristol City prospect

West Ham are on the lookout for potential transfer targets as the January window nears, and it appears they’ve got eyes on the next generation. The Sun states that WHUFC are interested in Joe Duncan, who is currently on loan at Southend United from Bristol City.

The 20-year-old, who was a surprise call-up to Craig Bellamy’s Wales squad for their UEFA Nations League fixtures against Norway and Portugal, has caught the eye in the National League. Duncan has earned rave reviews since moving to Roots Hall, playing a key role in them sitting in 3rd place.

Ipswich Town are also said to be monitoring Duncan’s situation, with the shot-stopper’s contract set to expire at the end of the season. The Bristol City academy graduate is yet to make a senior first-team appearance at Ashton Gate, having joined aged 11.

It is common for Football League sides to loan out their academy prospects to non-league outfits to give them a taste of regular first-team action. Duncan is following that very path, and interest from West Ham and Ipswich Town shows his success is not going unnoticed.

Penning his first professional contract at Bristol City in the summer of 2023, the two-time Wales U21 international flourished at Tiverton Town and Farborough.

With Tiverton, he was nominated for their Player of the Month award in each month he was there which speaks volumes about the impact he had in Devon. A stint with Farnborough last season brought similar success, helping to retain their National League South status after arriving in March.

Making the step-up to the National League with Southend United felt like the perfect stepping stone ahead of the new season and he has impressed at Roots Hall, despite only keeping one clean sheet.

A first senior call-up to the Wales squad indicates the potential Duncan has though, featuring on the bench in their defeat to Norway on Saturday.

Discussion is rife around what business West Ham may do in the January transfer window, but adding another young ‘keeper to their ranks shouldn’t be top of the to-do list.

Although the goalkeeping department is an area of concern due to the uncertain future of Alphonse Areola and poor performances of Mads Hermansen, the Hammers have plenty of talent waiting in the wings.

Finlay Herrick has shown his worth in the limited minutes he has been given, while Mason Terry continues to aid his development with loan moves in non-league, signing for Yeovil Town last week.

A January addition between the sticks will be paramount for Scott Parker to help shore up a leaky defence, but it is much more likely to be a senior figure.

Duncan is undoubtedly a fine talent who could become a big name in the years to come. However, a move to WHUFC may not provide a clear pathway to first-team football, given the young shot-stoppers already in their ranks.

#2128. “It is an honour” – Yormark speaks after Milan Sport & Entertainment CEO appointment

Earlier today, AC Milan were introduced to a new name, thanks to the creation of a new company: Milan Sports & Entertainment.

The international break is never really a period of major news, yet Milan broke that spell today. Indeed, RedBird Capital Partners published a statement about a new company formed to oversee all of the Rossoneri’s business.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

A centralised company that would allow everything Diavolo to be kept in one place is a major step forward for the club and city. San Donato was mentioned as an important focus for the new company, but the headline will remain the footballing operation.

To help with the project, Gerry Cardinale – who spoke about the decision – has appointed Brett Yormark to be the CEO of the company and reports suggest that he will join in January.

Speaking today, via MilanNews, Yormark admitted it was an honour to be part of the Milan family.

“It is an honour and a privilege to take on this role in a city with Milan’s history and passion for sports. I understand the responsibility that comes with leading an organisation tied to institutions that mean so much to their fans and the communities they serve.

“Throughout my career, I have helped evolve sports organisations, expand their reach, and create new opportunities. I look forward to leveraging this experience to realise RedBird’s vision for MSE.

“I am thrilled to work alongside Gerry, RedBird, and our colleagues in Milan to transform this vision into concrete, lasting results.”

#2127. Lazio, Inter su Provstgaard: Chivu lo vuole a gennaio, ma il prezzo frena l'assalto

Il difensore danese piace all'Inter su indicazione di Chivu, ma il club biancoceleste non fa sconti e fissa il prezzo a 40 milioni.

Secondo quanto riportato da La Repubblica, l'Inter avrebbe messo nel mirino Oliver Provstgaard, giovane difensore rivelazione della Lazio, su esplicita richiesta di Cristian Chivu per rinforzare la retroguardia. Per il club biancoceleste, tuttavia, il centrale rappresenta ormai un punto fermo: grazie al lavoro svolto sotto la guida di Gennaro Gattuso, il giocatore ha compiuto un netto salto di qualità sfornendo in questa stagione prove di grande solidità.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il centrale danese ha conquistato gli osservatori nerazzurri grazie a prestazioni di altissimo livello, convincendo la dirigenza milanese a considerarlo il profilo ideale per la difesa. Chivu vede in lui la soluzione perfetta per un reparto che necessita di interventi mirati in ottica presente e futura.

Tuttavia, l'operazione si prospetta complessa: Claudio Lotito non ha intenzione di svendere il danese  e per privarsi del difensore a gennaio chiederebbe una cifra vicina ai 40 milioni di euro. Per questo motivo, l'Inter continua a monitorare anche alternative come Mario Hermoso, in scadenza con la Roma nel giugno 2027, pronto a inserirsi in caso di mancato accordo per il rinnovo.

#2126. Recalcati sicuro: “Chivu, panchina d’oro meritata. Fabregas con lui ha sempre perso male”

In diretta alla Radio di Tuttomercatoweb si è proseguito il dibattito delle ultime ore, incentrato sull'assegnazione della panchina d'oro per la stagione 2025-26. Ad essere eletto come miglior allenatore di Serie A della passata stagione è stato Cristian Chivu, ma a detta di molti il titolo lo avrebbe meritato maggiormente Cesc Fabregas. In tal senso il giornalista e grande tifoso dell'Inter Christian Recalcati non ha avuto dubbi in merito: "Non c'è neanche da farmela questa domanda, la meritava Chivu per come è arrivato; ricordo che si parlava di quinto o sesto, mentre alla fine ha vinto sia campionato che Coppa Italia. Fabregas contro Chivu poi ha perso sempre e anche male. Lo spagnolo diventerà un bravissimo allenatore, ma guardando all'anno scorso la panchina d'oro l'ha meritata Chivu".

Entrando più nello specifico sul tema Inter, Recalcati si è poi espresso in merito proprio su alcune dichiarazioni di Chivu, il quale ha affermato di non temere nessuno in ottica scudetto: "La Roma è sicuramente attrezzata per lottare fino alla fine, ma l'Inter ha chiaramente qualcosa in più e al massimo deve temere sé stessa. I giallorossi hanno fatto un mercato senza vendere i giocatori principali, per qualcuno è una macchina da guerra, ma nello scontro diretto non ho visto una vittoria schiacciante della Roma. I nerazzurri ovviamente non hanno già vinto lo scudetto, ma rimangono i favoriti. La Lazio è davanti, il Milan e il Como sono vicine, ma l'Inter ha qualcosa in più. Negli ultimi anni del resto quando si è vinto lo scudetto è stato per propri meriti, mentre quando lo hanno vinto altri è stato per propri demeriti".

A proposito di Como, questa sarà per la banda di Fabregas una stagione da tutto o da niente? "È un'ottima squadra e gioca molto bene, ma probabilmente non è ancora pronta per il campionato e in Champions League, pur facendo bene, può puntare massimo agli ottavi o ai quarti".

Infine Recalcati ha "risposto" ad alcune dichiarazioni dell'ex calciatore Antonio Cassano. Il talento di Bari vecchia del resto, dopo l'ultima sconfitta dell'Italia contro il Belgio, aveva appellato la squadra azzurra come scarsa. Un pensiero non condiviso dal giornalista intervenuto nel corso del programma Maracana: "Dopo la prima partita contro il Belgio tutti dicevano che non si poteva andare da nessuna parte. Quella dell'altro ieri è stata gran batosta, ma dieci punti in Nations League rappresentano un ottimo cammino. Abbiamo pur sempre dei giocatori che fanno titolari in Premier League. Se dobbiamo paragonarli a Maldini, Nesta ecc fermiamoci subito, ma io penso che stiamo ripartendo e che qualcosa può nascere. Vedo un'Italia che sta crescendo".

#2125. EFL expert on whether Derby County can survive ‘difficult’ Championship relegation battle

Derby County are positioned in 23rd place in the table after their first 15 fixtures. DCFC are in the drop zone along with Preston North End and Burnley and they are five points from safety behind Watford and Lincoln City.

The Rams won 3-1 at home against Bolton Wanderers last time out before the international break. Ryan Nyambe, Jack Robinson and Rhian Brewster got their goals against the Trotters at Pride Park as they secured a valuable three points.

Elek has delivered his verdict on John Eustace’s side on the Not The Top 20 Podcast: “Relegation standard, they have to be. It comes with all the caveats, he’s a manager we like in John Eustace, their injuries are one of, if not the main reason, they are as poor as they have been this season. They did put in a much better performance last time out, we saw them win a game.”

He added: “Of course they can (get out of trouble), when (Patrick) Agyemang gets back he will give them so much more from an attacking perspective. They have so many defenders out. Matt Clarke probably being the most important one, he’s set to come back relatively soon which will improve them. But even so, from this moment on, whether it’s Eustace or whoever else, if someone can keep them up it will be one hell of an effort because they’ve really made their task pretty difficult to stay up given the way they have started.”

Derby County’s victory over Bolton Wanderers was a massive one and has eased some pressure off Eustace. However, DCFC’s head coach still has heat on him.

The Rams are back in action this weekend as they aim to build some momentum and claw their way closer to safety. They face a tricky away trip to St Mary’s to face Southampton and they will need to be at their best to get something against the promotion chasing Saints.

Eustace joined his current club back in February 2025 and left Blackburn Rovers in the process. They were in a relegation battle when he joined and he managed to keep them up.

The ex-Birmingham City man had Derby County competing for the play-offs in the last campaign. They missed out on the final day at the expense of eventual winners Hull City after they lost to Sheffield United.

Derby County’s squad has been plagued with injuries this term, as Elek has alluded to. DCFC need to get their injured players back fit as soon as possible.

Patrick Agyemang will give them more firepower up top and will be itching to return. The Rams also remain without defenders Matt Clarke, Eiran Cashin and Craig Forsyth, hence why they have delved into the free agent market over recent times to bring in experienced duo Robinson and Shane Duffy.

If the Rams were to sack Eustace in the near future, there are plenty of available managers who they could consider. Rob Edwards and Steve Cooper seem the most likely candidates, whilst the likes of Sean Dyche, Steven Gerrard, Paul Heckingbottom and Gary Rowett are unattached.

#2124. Lazio, Mandas sfida Svilar nelle quote per il miglior portiere

Il giallorosso è favorito a 2,50, mentre il portiere biancoceleste lo insegue a 5,00 su Goldbet e Sisal.

La Lazio di Christos Mandas e la Roma di Mile Svilar si sfidano anche nelle quote per il titolo di miglior portiere: il giallorosso è favorito a 2,50, mentre il biancoceleste lo insegue a 5,00 su Goldbet e Sisal. Il confronto accompagna quello tra le due squadre nella prima parte della Serie A.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La Lazio guida la classifica e la Roma è terza, a due punti di distanza. Le due squadre sono fra le protagoniste di questo avvio di campionato: una situazione che richiama le stagioni tra la fine degli anni Novanta e i primi anni Duemila, quando il derby della Capitale era anche una sfida per lo scudetto.

Nel confronto tra i portieri, Svilar è in testa alla lavagna a 2,50. Il portiere è ormai una certezza e un punto fermo della Roma. Mandas, tornato titolare dopo una stagione ai margini del progetto Sarri-bis, segue a 5,00.

#2123. Belgian journalist admits Sylla may be ‘more inclined’ to move to England: “His dream destination”

AC Milan have been heavily interested in Massire Sylla recently, but it seems they may struggle to sign him, let alone in January.

It’s no secret that Milan want to sign a central defender and whilst Goncalo Inacio is the leading name, he’s not the only one in the mix. Instead, there are several public names of interest and Sylla is one of them.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

First linked by Matteo Moretto, his name has become more popular in reports linking the Rossoneri to defenders and he seems like a concrete target for the January mercato. Yet, it seems like it will be difficult to bring him to San Siro.

Speaking to MilanNews, Het Nieuwsblad journalist Tjorven Messiaen looked at Massire Sylla and what his future had in store.

In Italy, there’s been talk of AC Milan’s interest in Sylla. What’s being said about him in Belgium?

“I saw that AC Milan have linked him with him. In Belgium, we discovered him through social media, and the USG hasn’t confirmed anything yet. It’s possible, however, that AC Milan has drawn attention to the boy, because he’s playing great football.

“His move to AC Milan is unlikely to materialise in January, however, as both Sylla and the club would be more inclined to listen to offers from the Premier League. He’s the kind of player who would be a perfect fit for the English league.”

What are his main strengths and best qualities?

“He’s young, he’s good in duels, he’s quick. He’s also very good at ball control. He’s a complete defender; he has the potential to become one of the best, and he’d be a perfect fit in the Premier League, which, among other things, is Sylla’s dream destination.”

What aspects of his game still need improvement?

“It’s hard to say, especially since his growth in recent months has been impressive. I’d say he needs to gain more experience, which will come simply by playing. So I don’t see many things he needs to improve to take the next step in his career.”

Do you think Union Saint-Gilloise (USG) would be willing to let him leave in January, or do you think any transfer discussions will be postponed until the summer?

“I don’t think USG will let him leave in January. The only possible scenario, which I don’t think will happen, but which is the only solution anyway, is for USG to sign him in January but keep him at least until the end of the season, especially since the club never sells its best players in January.

“I also think interested clubs could spend a lot of money, but Union won’t let him leave in January.”

Could you describe him in three adjectives?

“Quick, physical, and full of character, because he’s someone who always has problems when he has to make decisions.”

“Considering his development, I’d say Pacho. He came from nowhere in Belgium, developed, moved to Frankfurt, and then ended up at PSG. In Sylla’s case, I think he’ll go straight to the Premier League. He has everything it takes to be perfect for this league.”

He’s still very young. Do you think he’s ready to play for a big club in a big city like Milan?

“He’s still very young, but he’s humble, calm, and we mustn’t forget that he arrived last year, in January, from the Norwegian second division. He played very little last year, but he’s very strong mentally and has started this season as if he’s been playing with this team for years.

“I can’t imagine USG without Sylla today, especially since we’re talking about one of the best defenders in the league. At the end of the season, he’ll make a significant leap in his career, but in my opinion it won’t be AC ​​Milan because he’s waiting for the Premier League. And from what I know, several English clubs are already ready to make him a player.

“So AC Milan could give him a try, but it will require a big project and a lot of money.”

We shall have to see how January develops and whether he could be sold on the project. However, it certainly doesn’t bode well from these comments.

#2118. Highly-rated Reading youngster agrees out-of-window loan move

Reading youngster Tyler Sackey has featured only in cup matches for the club over the past three seasons. His three senior appearances this term have come in the EFL Trophy, and the 20-year-old has impressed in his outings.

Aside from his EFL Trophy appearances, Sackey has played for the U21s in Premier League 2, operating from central midfield. He impressed last term, and now looks set for a chance to prove his abilities on the senior stage away from Leam Richardson‘s squad.

Pete O’Rourke states that Reading have agreed to send Sackney on loan to National League side Aldershot Town. It will give him a chance to play frequent first-team football for the first time in his promising career.

Sackey signed a new contract at the Berkshire outfit in the summer, keeping him tied down until 2029.

On Sackey’s senior debut, he scored in a 3-1 EFL Trophy win against West Ham United U21s. He also scored against Walsall in the same competition in the 2024/25 season.

The Reading prospect has recent experience against National League sides, including scoring against Woking in a pre-season friendly with a composed left-footed finish this summer.

Sackey offers a creative presence in central midfield and further forward as a No. 10. He has made 158 successful passes in the Premier League 2 this term while winning 59.5% of his duels and having 270 touches. Combined with 27 defensive contributions, it shows the Royals youngster’s well-rounded game and his work rate out of possession in winning the ball back and showing to receive the ball.

This will be Sackey’s first chance at regular senior minutes, so he needs to be given time to adjust to the physical demands of the game and settle into his role in the team and dressing room at Aldershot.

Reading aren’t afraid to trust youthful creative midfielders, so Sackey has the potential to pave his way into the first team.

Charlie Savage is a prime example of this, following a youth career with Manchester United and a brief loan with Forest Green Rovers. He quickly became a key influence, and Bristol City loanee Josh Stokes has been entrusted with a similar role, having provided three assists so far this season.

Sackey will hope to impress with Aldershot Town to soon replicate those performances for his parent club.

#2116. Zabatta: “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport, una grande festa per tutta la Campania”

Zabatta: “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport, una grande festa per tutta la Campania”

Presentata in Consiglio regionale l’iniziativa del 22 novembre alla Mostra d’Oltremare. Una giornata ad ingresso libero tra campioni, scuole, associazioni e cittadini.

Una giornata per riunire il movimento sportivo campano, valorizzarne i protagonisti e promuovere una pratica sportiva sempre più accessibile e inclusiva. È “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport 2026”, l’iniziativa promossa dall’Assessorato allo Sport della Regione Campania, presentata questa mattina nella Sala Caduti di Nassiriya del Consiglio regionale.

L’appuntamento è per domenica 22 novembre alla Mostra d’Oltremare, Padiglione 3B , con incontri, premiazioni, attività dimostrative e occasioni di confronto tra istituzioni, federazioni, associazioni, scuole, atleti e cittadini.

Alla conferenza stampa hanno partecipato l’assessora regionale allo Sport Fiorella Zabatta, il presidente del Consiglio regionale Massimiliano Manfredi, Sandro Cuomo, campione olimpico nella spada a squadre ad Atlanta 1996, e l’atleta paralimpica Angela Procida.

“Vogliamo che sia una grande festa dello sport campano, un’occasione per dare spazio a ogni disciplina, a ogni talento e a ogni storia. Lo sport è passione, sacrificio e inclusione, una palestra di vita che unisce generazioni e crea comunità. Con questa iniziativa, unica nel suo genere in Campania, vogliamo riunire chi vive lo sport ogni giorno: dagli atleti alle associazioni, dalle scuole alle federazioni, fino alle famiglie. Il nostro obiettivo è costruire uno sport davvero accessibile, perché non deve essere un privilegio legato alle possibilità economiche, ma un diritto e un’opportunità per tutti”, ha detto l’ assessora regionale allo Sport Fiorella Zabatta.

”Gli Stati generali dello sport devono segnare l’avvio di una nuova stagione di impegno e di politiche concrete per la Campania. Lo sport non è un lusso né un privilegio, ma un diritto e un fondamentale strumento di crescita, salute, educazione e inclusione sociale. La nostra sfida è garantire a ogni cittadino, indipendentemente dal territorio in cui vive o dalle proprie condizioni economiche e personali, la possibilità di praticarlo. Servono investimenti negli impianti, sostegno alle associazioni e alle società sportive e una particolare attenzione ai giovani, alle persone con disabilità e alle fasce più fragili. Il Consiglio regionale è pronto a fare la propria parte: dobbiamo trasformare le tante energie del mondo sportivo in una visione comune e in azioni concrete. Perché investire nello sport significa investire nel futuro, nella coesione e nella qualità della vita della nostra comunità. La Campania ha bisogno di una politica dello sport che non lasci indietro nessuno”, ha aggiunto il presidente del Consiglio regionale Massimiliano Manfredi.

Il programma del 22 novembre inizia alle 9.30 von l’accoglienza dei partecipanti; alle 10.30 apriranno gli stand delle Federazioni sportive campane. Alle 11 il convegno “Sport e scuola: educazione, inclusione e benessere” darà il via alla sessione mattutina, che proseguirà con le premiazioni di “Napoli Capitale Europea dello Sport” e i riconoscimenti alle scuole, alle associazioni e alle realtà che si sono distinte nella promozione della pratica sportiva.

Alle 18 si terrà il convegno “Presente e Futuro dello Sport in Campania”, seguito dalle premiazioni della Presidenza del Consiglio regionale e dai riconoscimenti ai grandi protagonisti dello sport, tra cui la campionessa olimpica di ginnastica artistica Manila Esposito, altri atleti e rappresentanti delle Federazioni sportive.

Per tutta la giornata sarà possibile visitare gli stand, conoscere e provare diverse discipline sportive e partecipare alle attività dimostrative. Alle 20 è previsto lo show cooking “La dieta dello sportivo”.

L’iniziativa è organizzata dall’Assessorato allo Sport della Regione Campania, con la partecipazione del CONI Campania, del Comitato Italiano Paralimpico (CIP), di Sport e Salute e di Padelness e Sport, con il supporto operativo di Sviluppo Campania. L’ingresso è libero .

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2115. Cardinale says MSE will give Milan ‘financial muscle’ and reiterates: “I will never sell”

Gerry Cardinale has commented on the launch of AC Milan’s new holding company, Milan Sports & Entertainment (MSE), stating that he will never sell the club.

Yesterday, it was confirmed that RedBird had launched the new holding company to ‘consolidate RedBird’s Milan-based investments in sports, infrastructure, and entertainment’. From the new stadium to the NBA Europa project, there are big plans for the company.

Brett Yormark has been appointed as the CEO of MSE after impressing as the Commissioner of the Big 12 Conference (college sports), labelling it ‘an honour’ to get the position. It doesn’t mean that we will see less of Cardinale though, quite the opposite.

In an interview with Gazzetta dello Sport this morning, Cardinale outlined exactly what he wants to achieve with MSE and explained why he will never sell the club. Silvio Berlusconi 2.0, as he calls it, with the goal of taking Milan back to the very top.

Cardinale, what benefits will AC Milan gain from the holding company?

“The driving force behind deals like this is to provide AC Milan with the financial muscle needed to compete in the transfer market and secure the best players in the world.

“I will transform the club’s financial profile with the resources that will come from the stadium, the San Donato Arena, and the other activities we are working on with NBA Europe.

“I’m doing everything I can to ensure that, when we want a player, no one can tell me, ‘You can’t sign him because you can’t afford him.’ Currently, only Premier League clubs are able to invest such sums and secure top players.

“If I want to bring AC Milan back to the top of European competition, not just in Italy, we need to figure out how to compete with the world’s top clubs.”

This project confirms that your commitment to Milan is long-term…

“Yes. I will never sell AC Milan. Never. And this is a project to get them back to winning ways. I’m not willing to tolerate it taking a long time to achieve this. I want to do it as soon as possible. We’ll see how quickly…

“My goal is to consistently win Scudetti and, of course, return to the Champions League. Milan Sports & Entertainment represents the foundation to make all this possible.”

The connection with Milan deserves a separate mention: RedBird and the city are now even more connected…

“This is the legacy we will leave in Milan. The stadium, obviously, represents a huge legacy that will transform our financial profile, but then there’s also the San Donato development project and the rest.

“If we want AC Milan to become great again, we need to close the revenue gap with the Premier League clubs. When I bought the club four years ago, I used the expression ‘Berlusconi 2.0’ and no one understood what I meant. Now perhaps we are starting to see it.”

“Everyone thought I was making an arrogant, self-centred comparison between me and Berlusconi, but that wasn’t my point. What I was trying to explain was that, just as Berlusconi transformed AC Milan and Serie A, we want to be able, through our ownership, to lead the club into a new era.

“We need to do it in the same way Berlusconi did, albeit in a modern way. I’m used to working a certain way because that’s how I do things, at the highest levels worldwide and in America.”

Could your “recipe” also be valid for Serie A?

“Of course, even for Serie A. In my opinion, it wouldn’t have been a good deal to sell 30% of the league’s international TV rights when JP Morgan made that offer. That’s not how Serie A can return to the top of Europe.

“It needs to be done through business planning and by creating every opportunity to generate new revenue. Like we will do with Milan Sports & Entertainment.

“We will be the first to build a stadium since 2011, and that’s a huge achievement. I started working on it when I bought AC Milan, and finally… the right time has arrived.”

Do you think others will follow you on this path?

“No. Some may do it, others may not. We know what it takes to innovate and change. To avoid continuing to practice Einstein’s definition of insanity, according to which everyone wants a different outcome, but doesn’t want to do anything different to get it.”

How long do you think it will take to complete the other projects that will be part of the new holding company?

“I think the NBA is negotiating with the Euroleague, but one way or another, if they can’t reach an agreement, NBA Europe will still launch. Perhaps the first batch of new franchises for Europe will be announced at the end of the year or in early 2027.

“We will see when that happens… We own the land in San Donato, and if I’m granted the Milan franchise, I will be able to start construction fairly quickly because we already have planning permission for a 20,000-seat arena.

“We will also continue to explore opportunities and hold discussions within the tennis world and to organise a tournament in Milan. Finally, we are already making progress in building and launching a hospitality and facility management company.”

Does your plan include organising and managing hospitality services for other clubs’ stadiums as well, not just the new San Siro?

“Yes, the model is the company I created in 2009, called Legends Hospitality. I created it with the New York Yankees and the Dallas Cowboys. Now that company is worth several billion dollars.

“We not only sell premium seats, suites, merchandise, and hospitality, food, and beverage services for Yankee Stadium and Cowboys Stadium, but also for other clubs. I would like to adopt the same model here, probably in partnership with another club.”

Not many football clubs in the world have established an organisation like the one you are leading…

“Exactly. My ambition is to compete with the best clubs in the world. I want to do so thanks to my global vision of the world of sports and entertainment.

“There aren’t many multi-sports platforms in the world that also own some of their own facilities, and we want to add AC Milan to that list. To compete with them and then be better.”

In an effort to generate new revenue, are you also considering a tour for AC Milan next summer? Will that still be Australia or the United States?

“We have already discussed it with Amorim, and I will just tell you that if we play friendlies abroad, they will be in the United States next summer. If we do… We haven’t decided yet, and the decision will be up to Ruben.

“It will depend on what’s best for the team: playing friendlies abroad must add value to the group, not bring in a couple of million for the club. Those extra two million will no longer be our goal, as they were under the previous management. That won’t happen again under this ownership.

“Everything we organise in terms of friendlies will be carefully considered with our coach. Every move. And we won’t participate in tours organised by others. We put our players first. We want to help them prepare well to win. That’s our goal, and we aim to achieve it as soon as possible.”

Gerry is right. You are wrong. Look it up.

The English Premiere League makes about FOUR TIMES as much money in TV broadcast rights as Serie A does – €4.6 billion per season (£3.84 billion – £1.68 billion domestic, £2.17 billion international).

Serie A’s TV broadcast rights give it €1.144 billion per season (€894 million domestic, €250 million international)

Total stadium revenue among all the clubs in the English Premier League is also about 2.3 x higher than total stadium revenue for all the clubs in Serie A – over £1billion per season in the EPL (€1.3 billion) compared with €435.4 million per season in Serie A.

It was not always this way. In the 1980s, English football was beset with old, rotting, unsafe stadiums, hooliganism, and low TV revenues.

In 1992, the First Division clubs broke away from the rest of the English Football League (EFL) to form the Premiere League, and began negotiating TV contracts on their own, as well as embarking on a program of enlarging and upgrading their football stadiums. The richest teams began building completely new, large modern stadiums with premium suites, etc.

Meanwhile, nothing much has changed in Italy – most of the stadiums remain municipal owned and small and old.

Ticket prices in the EPL are also generally on average about twice as expensive as in Italy.

So yeah, Serie A had its glory days in the 1990s, as wealthy magnates like Berlusconi infused their personal wealth into their clubs. I remember Fox carried Serie A football on TV in the United States back then. If you wanted to watch the English Premiere League, it was a bit of a struggle to find the matches on TV in the U.S back in the 1990s.

All that changed as the Premiere League steadily built up its international marketing and negotiated ever improved TV contracts. It began to require clubs that were promoted up to the EPL to upgrade their stadiums to meet a certain minimum seating capacity.

And so, today, when Italians complain about how Italy and Serie A can no longer compete with the English Premiere League for the best players, how the EPL hugely overpays for all its star players, the reason is that the EPL makes a $HIT TON MORE MONEY than Serie A does, across the board, in TV income (the biggest factor for the entire league), and stadium income.

That’s why Gerry wanted a new and better stadium, and why he has often talked about Serie A needing to do better marketing and improve its TV contracts.

You can’t get the best players in the world if you can’t compete financially with the richest and most watched football league in the world, which happens to be the English Premiere League right now.

Here in the US, EPL matches are carried by NBC Sports by subscription (about $130-$200 a year). NBC pays the Premiere League $450 million per season to carry English football on TV in the USA.

Serie A matches are carried by Paramount/CBS in the USA (a $90 per year subscription – it went up from $60 after the Ellison Oligarchy mergers). CBS had been paying Serie A $75.8 million per year for those TV rights. However, Serie A with its great marketing and negotiating expertise was unable to improve or renew that contract and so Serie A is currently just going year to year with CBS and getting less than $30 million a year.

Do you see the difference now in the major sources of income between the Premiere League and Serie A??

So yeah, absolutely, Gerry is right to complain about needing to get Serie A some better negotiators and marketers to improve on its TV contracts.

#2114. What next for Blackburn Rovers as Tony Mowbray steps back? New boss stance, impact on players and more

It marks the second time Mowbray has done so, temporarily withdrawing from his position at Birmingham City in February 2024 after being diagnosed with bowel cancer.

The news has been met with an outpouring of support from across English football, and deservedly so. Mowbray is one of the most universally liked figures in our game, and all hope to see him recover speedily.

Blackburn Rovers fans have sent their best wishes and got behind Mowbray first, of course. However, the news has understandably led to questions over what happens next at Ewood Park.

At the time of writing, it appears Blackburn Rovers will not be appointing a new head coach – not permanently, anyway. The wording of the club’s statement confirming Mowbray’s situation said he is ‘stepping back’, not stepping down, leaving, or anything similar.

First-team coaches Damien Johnson and Mike Dodds are taking over duties for the time being, and no further statement will be made, the club says.

The situation played out similarly upon Mowbray’s withdrawal from duties at Birmingham City. His long-term assistant Mark Venus took charge in his absence before the boss took formal medical leave and Gary Rowett was appointed on an interim basis.

This is a new and developing situation, but Rovers’ statement indicates a new boss will not be coming in at this moment in time. Trusted local reporter Elliott Jackson has since stated on X that the club ‘are certainly not looking for a successor right now’.

Without arguing semantics but to add clarity, I’m told Tony Mowbray has not ‘resigned’ or ‘stepped down’.He’s ‘stepped back’ to take a period to focus on his health. How long is that for? We don’t know.Club are certainly not looking for a successor right now. #Rovers🌹 https://t.co/9M8aWSEgsx

Mowbray cuts a hugely popular figure at Ewood Park. After relegation in 2016/17, he led them back from League One and consolidated their place back in the Championship before moving on at the end of his contract in 2022.

There were some reservations over his return though. It felt like an easy and crowd-pleasing move, but some questioned whether Mowbray could replicate his previous success after his absence and ill-fated second stint as West Brom boss.

However, Mowbray quickly made his presence felt at Blackburn Rovers, and they have overachieved in the first 15 games of the campaign. A side widely touted as relegation candidates now sits 13th in the table, three points off the play-offs and with big wins over top sides like West Ham, Millwall and Middlesbrough.

Mowbray has brought forward-thinking back to Ewood Park. It has seen creative aces like Todd Cantwell and Dapo Afolayan thrive, and star striker Andri Gudjohnsen has benefitted massively. Furthermore, the boss’ track record with developing youngsters has helped Kristi Montgomery come on leaps and bounds.

The 62-year-old has given Rovers fans something to celebrate again after tough years with poor football and growing frustration towards the ownership. Of course, hostility towards the hierarchy has not waned, but the quality of the football on display and positivity surrounding Mowbray’s return have made for a welcome change.

This is an unforeseen development that Blackburn Rovers have no choice but to deal with. Losing Mowbray, even if only temporarily, is a big blow, and it will impact the players.

Johnson and Dodds will focus on offering continuity. It will be smart to stick to Mowbray’s methods and a similar tactical plan, but they must be prepared to make bold decisions and necessary changes if form does falter.

Then, there’s the responsibility of the club hierarchy. It appears an external successor is not going to be named, but if Mowbray can not return to the dugout, the likes of Balaji Rao, Steve Curwood and Rudy Gestede have to be prepared with a plan to ensure it doesn’t derail their season.

When Birmingham City were met with the same situation, their initial action to promote Venus as interim boss did not work. Then, they acted too late to bring in an external replacement, and Rowett could not save them from relegation.

It is an awful predicament for Rovers, but they must be prepared for all scenarios to protect the club.

In the short term, though, Johnson, Dodds, the rest of the coaching staff and the Blackburn Rovers squad will be determined to deliver a derby day win over rivals Burnley for Mowbray as he recovers.

#2111. Barnsley developments hand Sheffield United & Derby County clear Reyes Cleary boost

Barnsley, Reyes Cleary, Sheffield United, and Derby County could all become central to an interesting January transfer situation, with the talented winger attracting attention from higher up the football pyramid.

Reyes Cleary has emerged as one of the most exciting attacking players in League One with Barnsley, and it is hardly surprising that Championship clubs are monitoring his situation again after having interest in the 22-year-old in the summer.

Both Sheffield United and Derby County have reason to strengthen their attacking options, while Barnsley face an increasingly important period in their own campaign. There could be some significant developments at Oakwell over the coming weeks ahead of January.

Reyes Cleary is arguably one of the most exciting and explosive wide players in League One, so it is little surprise that the likes of Sheffield United, Derby, and Bristol City have been credited with an interest in securing his services.

It's evident that he has the individual quality to make the step up to the Championship and would represent an exciting addition for any of the sides keen on him at a higher level.

However, Barnsley's latest defeat to Wigan Athletic has made their league position even more precarious, with the threat of relegation to League Two becoming increasingly difficult to ignore.

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

That could have significant implications for Cleary's future. Barnsley adopted a hardball stance during the summer and priced interested clubs out of a move, but their current predicament makes that approach considerably harder to justify.

Cleary might be vital to their survival hopes, but they cannot necessarily expect to retain him on their terms if Championship interest intensifies in January. Barnsley are now second-bottom and three points off safety, albeit with games in hand on some of the sides above them.

Barnsley deserve considerable credit for identifying Cleary's potential in the first place. He had shown promise during his time with Hartlepool United in the National League, but not many League One clubs would have taken the gamble of recruiting somebody two divisions higher and backing him to make the step up.

It has proved to be an inspired decision, although Barnsley now face a difficult situation with just 18 months remaining on his contract come January. Sheffield United and Derby are unlikely to be the only Championship clubs interested, either, with Bristol City also keen.

West Bromwich Albion should also have serious regrets about allowing Cleary to leave as early as they did. He looks every bit a player capable of establishing himself in the Championship, and a return to The Hawthorns could even be something worth exploring if they recognise their previous mistake.

That said, Barnsley have to be braced for offers and interest again, with it becoming increasingly to stand in the player's way if he is determined to test himself in the second tier. That said, Barnsley will be desperate to keep hold of arguably their best player.

There is, of course, another side to this argument. If Barnsley cannot turn their fortunes around and ultimately suffer relegation to League Two, Cleary's market value could fall considerably for clubs like Derby and Sheffield United, particularly with only 12 months remaining on his contract next summer.

From a financial perspective, January might just be their best opportunity to secure a substantial fee. However, selling him could also be a grave sporting mistake, too. Cleary is arguably Barnsley's most talented player and possesses the individual quality to help drag them away from the relegation places.

Losing that sort of match-winner midway through a difficult campaign could make an already challenging situation considerably worse. The Tykes have to weigh up the financial benefits of cashing in against the very real possibility that they need Cleary more than ever to preserve their League One status.

There are only three players with more than three goal involvements for Barnsley in all competitions this season, including Vimal Yoganathan with five, as well as both Cameron McGeehan and Cleary with nine apiece. No one can better Cleary's three assists, either.

Scarlett is very much in the developmental years of his career. His early involvement at Championship level does not mean he is a fixed member of Stephan’s squad yet, especially with previously sidelined players like Kolli and Ilias Chair back in the fold, while Rumarn Burrell nears his comeback.

However, the Hoops are more likely to be able to offer senior minutes and a quicker ascent to regular first-team selection. It means a potential Scarlett move could come down to his personal preference – does he want top coaching in elite academy facilities? Or does he want to progress with more senior exposure?

#2108. Bradford City's prospective new owners could face a £10m stadium complication

There are rumours that Bradford City could be up for sale, but any prospective new buyers of the club should be warned that any purchase of the club could come with one significant complication.

For several seasons, it looked rather as though Bradford City were living in some sort of footballing purgatory. Trapped in League Two and watched by huge crowds for that division despite barely challenging for promotion, the club seemed stuck in a loop which was proving extremely difficult to escape from.

At the end of the 2024-25 season, though, that run came to an end. Bradford were promoted back to League One following an absence of six years, and they marked their first season back by finishing in fourth place and securing a play-off place, where they lost 2-0 over two legs to Bolton Wanderers.

That form has broadly followed them into League One this season. The departure of manager Graham Alexander to Preston North End at the start of October was a big loss, and their form has dipped since then. Prior to that, though, the Bantams soared to the top of League One, again demonstrating that this is a club with untapped potential, and this in turn has reportedly attracted the interest of investors from abroad.

Recent reports from journalist Alan Nixon have confirmed that there is interest in buying Bradford City from a consortium of Thai investors who are seeking a route into the English game, with Nixon writing that, "Bradford could be for sale at the right price and the Thais, who have an intermediary with inside knowledge, are considering an official offer."

Stefan Rupp has been the public face of the club's ownership since 2016, and Rupp has been clear that he will only sell to the right buyer. Speaking to the Bradford Telegraph & Argus after the end of the 2025-26 season, Rupp said, "I'm the keeper of the keys and I would never hand it over to somebody where I have the impression he can't do any better."

How would you redevelop Valley Parade further to match Bradford City's growing ambitions? Let us know in the comments below!

On the surface, there are plenty of good reasons to have an interest in buying Bradford City. The days of them being underachievers in League Two already feel like a rapidly fading memory, and even though they've had a dip in form since the departure of Alexander, they remain one of the promotion contenders in League One again this season.

Crowds remain extremely healthy, at just shy of 20,000, and Valley Parade is a stadium which hosted Premier League football for two seasons at the turn of the century, representing a city of half a million people in which they remain the only professional football club.

But when The Athletic's Matt Slater reported on the possibility of the Bantams being sold in March 2026, he mentioned a potential sale price of around £10 million. This seems very low for an upwardly mobile League One club, but there's a very good reason for this modest valuation, and it comes down to what the club doesn't own.

The Bantams have been on the rise over the last couple of seasons, and they're now being linked with a takeover as they push for a Championship place.

In an era during which a Championship striker is having a £70 million price tag slapped on his head to deter predators, spending £10 million to buy an entire football club sounds like a bargain, but with the sale of any football club it can be beneficial to pay attention to the detail of what's actually being bought or sold.

And in the case of Bradford City, the club owns neither Valley Parade nor their training ground. Valley Parade belongs to former chairman Gordon Gibb's family pension fund. Bradford reportedly pay more than £500,000 annually to lease the ground, with the existing agreement due for renewal in 2028. Bradford have been giving consideration to possibly leaving the ground over the cost of the rent for at least a decade and a half.

And in the lower reaches of the EFL, the pure real estate value of the ground can be the biggest single cost aspect of any potential takeover. Without that, any new owner is essentially buying the player and staff contracts along with a license to operate the club in the EFL, and Bradford City, like the overwhelming majority of clubs, are not a profitable enterprise, with the last available set of company accounts showing a £3 million loss on a revenue of £8.6 million.

Earlier this year, the Bantams Supporters Trust entered into a consultation period with fans over what to do about the stadium, with a valuation of it put at between £8 million and £10 million. It's not known whether there are covenants limiting its use. But regardless of this, the Trust confirmed that they believe that the club's intention is to seek to continue to lease the stadium when that renewal comes up.

It would be advantageous to any new owners to take ownership of the ground itself. To do so would save them more than they're paying their highest-earning player every year, while its value would increase as land prices increase, and it would allow the club to redevelop the site more easily if they were looking to increase their revenues another way round.

But throwing the ground into the sale mix could double that original £10 million valuation, and although that would still represent stunning value for money were the club able to fight their way back to the Premier League, sale prices can vary wildly even within League One.

When Rob Couhig bought Reading from Dai Yongge in May 2025, for example, it was reported that the sale price was £30 million, but this included the Select Car Leasing Stadium and the club's training ground. But when Leicester City were put up for sale in September 2026, it was for a staggering £224 million, though this did include state-of-the-art training facilities, land around the stadium for redevelopment, and Leicester's Belgian sister club, OH Leuven.

Buying a football club at any level of the game can be a gamble, and the recent IFR report into the state of football finances stated that two-thirds of club have liabilities which exceed their assets and rely on owner funding to survive - making them technically insolvent - while only seven of the 116 professional clubs in the top five men’s divisions did not record a loss for the last available financial year.

All of this means that, while £10 million to buy a football may sound like a bargain, it's almost certain that this price is reflective of the extreme likelihood being that the cost to any new owner wouldn't end at that point. In the case of Bradford City, it wouldn't include the stadium itself, and this would likely double that price, while the new owner would also be on the hook for covering the club's losses, which at the last available count amounted to £3 million for one year.

Even at a sale price of £20 million to £30 million, buying Bradford City could prove to be a bargain. But so lop-sided are the finances of the majority of English football clubs that they may have to get the club all the way to the Premier League to turn a serious profit on their investment.
