The Swans have enjoyed an impressive start to the Championship season, as they sit sixth in the table after 15 games on 26 points, despite losing three of their last five encounters.

Matos' side were narrowly defeated before the November international break by current leaders, Birmingham City, at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park despite being a man down for the vast majority of the contest, and it may transpire that the loss in the West Midlands could be the 38-year-old's final game in charge.

Speculation involving the former Liverpool coach is nothing new to supporters in this part of South Wales in recent weeks, but one positive has emerged should Matos decide to move on to a third managerial role within the space of 12 months.

Matos was appointed by Swansea on November 24th of last year, three weeks after the Welsh outfit parted company with Alan Sheehan.

Jürgen Klopp's former Anfield assistant was lured from Maritimo through a reported £880,000 compensation fee, and subsequently transformed the Swans' form for the remainder of the season as they finished 11th - nine points outside the play-offs whilst boasting Golden Boot winner, Zan Vipotnik.

Having signed a one-year contract extension until June 2030 on July 31st, a strong first pre-season and summer transfer window has been backed up by a positive start, but it emerged on Monday that Strasbourg have approached Swansea over the potential appointment of Matos, with his compatriot, Hugo Oliveira, under pressure after just three wins this season.

Strasbourg are 13th in Ligue 1 at present, but would be forced to part with a significant sum of cash if they were to instill the highly-rated head coach at the Stade de la Meinau, and it has since been suggested that the figure being negotiated is in the region of £4m as Matos braces for his latest managerial change.

Ranking the Championship clubs based on how successful they've been since the start of 2001/02

Matos won 22 of his first 47 matches in charge of Swansea, boasting a win percentage of 47%, following on from a 54% total in just 13 matches at his previous employers, Maritimo.

It is a testament to the work put in by the Portuguese that clubs are looking to poach him away from Swansea, and that recently included the Premier League's bottom club, Crystal Palace.

The Eagles were recently credited with interest in the Portuguese and Middlesbrough head coach, Kim Hellberg, before opting to confirm Thomas Frank as Pierre Sage's replacement at Selhurst Park on Monday afternoon.

Despite losing Ethan Galbraith to Stoke City for £10m, Matos changed the outlook of his Swansea side with 11 new recruits, with the likes of Joseph Opoku, Moussa Yeo and Stephen Welsh, already earning rave reviews.

Such a strong recruitment drive came after the penning of Matos' contract extension, after which he explained his sudden connection towards all aspects of the club as he aimed to drive the Swans forward.

"From the moment I arrived here, I have sensed how powerful the connection between Swansea City and the fans and community is," Matos said.

"This is something very special, it is a feeling myself and the players love, and the desire to keep building that and sharing great moments together is one of the key motivations in extending my contract with the club."

#2104. How Dave Challinor honed his craft with Celtic, Brentford trips before taking Sheffield Wednesday job

Out-of-work managers often take the time out of the spotlight to evolve their coaching game, and this was no different for new Sheffield Wednesday boss Dave Challinor. Between leaving Stockport County and taking the SWFC job, the 51-year-old has revealed Celtic’s Martin O’Neill and Brentford boss Keith Andrews’ influences.

In an interview with The Star, Challinor revealed he had dinner with O’Neill, alongside watching his Celtic side train and play. On O’Neill, the Owls boss said: “He is a really inspirational character. He is a figurehead for that football club. The success he’s had at such a massive place is enormous.”

Challinor then toured Brentford’s training ground and took inspiration from how the Bees prepare for set pieces.

“Brentford, I suppose for any club in England, or across the world, really, just the way they go about it and the way they’ve done it from the very, very top, is unbelievable,” he said. “They are an inspiration not just to me as a manager, but I’d suggest football clubs the length and breadth of the country.”

Challinor has plenty of experience in the dugout, but for someone who has followed his trajectory, there is always more to learn. He has journeyed through the football pyramid from Colwyn Bay to Sheffield Wednesday in 16 years, so he can take valuable lessons from those who have pedigree at a higher level.

In terms of his managerial abilities as an individual, Challinor will have taken plenty from O’Neill at Celtic. The 74-year-old progressed through lower leagues like the Sheffield Wednesday boss before managing big clubs in top leagues, such as Leicester City, Celtic and Aston Villa.

The sheer amount of knowledge and experience O’Neill has gained over the years means he will have had some valuable nuggets of advice to pass down to Challinor.

As for Brentford, touring their training ground would have been more of a lesson in full club management, and what can help traditionally ‘smaller’ clubs compete with the elite. The Bees are not the wealthiest club by any means, but they are sustainably run from the top to the bottom, and meticulous training sessions and match preparation means they are now in their sixth season as a Premier League side.

Challinor will have taken valuable lessons from these trips, and it will only benefit SWFC.

David Storch has expressed that Sheffield Wednesday are just at the start of a long-term rebuild. Challinor was not appointed with immediate success in mind, he was brought in to lead a new era at Hillsborough.

Some of the Hillsborough faithful will expect a strong push for promotion from League One this season, and justifiably so. Despite Storch stating the need for patience, the squad is strong enough to emerge as a strong contender at the top end of an evenly matched third tier.

Challinor’s willingness to learn and take inspiration from other managers and model clubs is an excellent attribute. Despite the success he has found in the dugout already, his desire to grow as a boss is only a good thing for SWFC as they rebuild under his leadership.

#2103. Ruben Neves drops new 'crazy' Wolves claim

Former Wolves midfielder Rúben Neves has revealed he is looking forward to playing at the Cardiff City Stadium again, as he reflected on a ‘crazy’ 1-0 win over the Bluebirds earlier in his career.

The 29-year-old is currently playing for Al-Hilal, with the move to Saudi Arabia coming after six years at Wolves. Neves had a remarkable time at Molineux, initially joining the club from Porto when they were in the Championship.

Despite the risk, it was a move that worked out, as he starred for Nuno Espírito Santo’s side as they won the title in the second tier, before recording consecutive 7th-placed finishes in the Premier League.

Neves would ultimately make over 250 appearances in Old Gold, and his quality, leadership and loyalty means he is regarded as a modern-day legend at the club.

During that period, there were many high points for Wolves and Neves, and one of the standout moments came during the 2017/18 campaign, when Wanderers won the Championship title.

In April of that season, Nuno’s side made the trip to take on fellow promotion rivals Cardiff, and the two teams played out one of the most remarkable games in Championship history.

Neves had given the visitors the lead with a trademark free-kick, but Cardiff missed the chance to equalise in stoppage time when Gary Madine saw his effort saved by John Ruddy. Incredibly, the Bluebirds would be awarded another spot-kick, but Junior Hoilett hit the bar with the last kick of the game.

That prompted wild celebrations, with Cardiff boss Neil Warnock famously snubbing Nuno’s offer of a handshake after the game.

So, it’s a stadium that will hold many memories for all Wolves fans, and Neves is back in the Welsh capital with Portugal, as Jorge Jesus’ side prepare to take on the Dragons in the Nations League.

Inevitably, Neves was questioned about coming back to the Cardiff City Stadium, and he told BBC Wales that it remains one of the ‘craziest’ games he has ever been involved in.

“I remember (it) very well! It was a crazy match, one of the craziest matches in my life, and fortunately we won that match.

“I scored a free-kick, and then in the last minutes they had two penalties, they missed both penalties, and of course, after that there was a bit of confusion on the pitch because the emotions were high.

“But it was a fantastic match, and it’s a stadium that brings me good memories.”

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

This is nice for Wolves fans to hear, as they still love Neves, and it will be great for them to see that he still looks back on his time at the club fondly, with all supporters hoping that he might one day return for a second spell at some point before he retires.

Obviously, the Cardiff game is one that will live long in the memory, and it’s no exaggeration to say that it was an ending to a match that you rarely ever see.

It was a huge disappointment for Cardiff at the time, but, to their credit, the Bluebirds did manage to put the defeat behind them, as they went up automatically with Wolves - and they did actually beat them at home in the top flight.

What is the craziest match you have seen? Let us know in the comments below.

But, that night was pivotal for Wolves, and, even after everything Neves went on to achieve at the club, and in his career, it was clearly a game that stands out.

So, he will have fond memories of Cardiff, and Neves will hope to leave with another win with Portugal against Wales on Tuesday night.

#2102. Cardinale says pre-season is ‘up to Amorim’ as Milan will not chase ‘extra two million’

Gerry Cardinale has provided some refreshing comments on Milan’s pre-season plans for the future, confirming that money will no longer dictate where they go.

Over the last ten years or so, it has become far more common for big clubs to travel across the world for their pre-season campaigns. For example, Milan have been in Australia for two consecutive pre-seasons now, taking on Perth Glory and most recently Inter.

While it’s great to connect with local fan bases, the travel is demanding for the players. This summer, Milan played games in Glasgow (Scotland), Perth (Australia), Jakarta (Indonesia), and Wroclaw (Poland). resulting in a lot of travel.

Speaking about the pre-season in his long interview with Gazzetta dello Sport, Cardinale said that the upcoming pre-season planning will be decided by Ruben Amorim. Money will no longer dictate where they go. Instead, the best plan for preparing the players will be chosen.

In an effort to generate new revenue, are you also considering a tour for AC Milan next summer? Will that still be Australia or the United States?

“We have already discussed it with Amorim, and I will just tell you that if we play friendlies abroad, they will be in the United States next summer. If we do… We haven’t decided yet, and the decision will be up to Ruben.

“It will depend on what’s best for the team: playing friendlies abroad must add value to the group, not bring in a couple of million for the club. Those extra two million will no longer be our goal, as they were under the previous management. That won’t happen again under this ownership.

“Everything we organise in terms of friendlies will be carefully considered with our coach. Every move. And we won’t participate in tours organised by others. We put our players first. We want to help them prepare well to win. That’s our goal, and we aim to achieve it as soon as possible.”

#2101. Swindon Town tipped to take Ian Holloway action after Grimsby Town taunts

Swindon Town have been tipped to potentially part ways with experienced manager, Ian Holloway, after the Robins were defeated heavily by his former employers, Grimsby Town, at the County Ground on Saturday afternoon.

The Robins are currently 16th in League Two having taken 19 points from their first 15 matches, but have won just twice in their last 14 encounters across all competitions.

Saturday's 4-1 loss against the high-flying Mariners was also Swindon's third on the spin, having been eliminated from the FA Cup against League One side, MK Dons, as well as losing in the EFL Trophy to Plymouth Argyle.

Supporters in Wiltshire have grown increasingly frustrated with the club's recent situation, as the club have continued to stagnate in the fourth tier in the five years of Clem Morfuni's ownership.

Holloway recently surpassed two years in charge at the County Ground, but after finishing 12th in his first full season last term, there are concerns as to whether his team can show any signs of progress based on their recent form.

Holloway is, arguably, the EFL's most experienced manager at present, having taken charge at matches across all levels for 30 years, including Premier League spells with Blackpool and Crystal Palace.

The 63-year-old has gained a strong reputation for various incidents over time, and this season, that has been no exception for the Swindon boss.

After slamming Manchester City for their alleged financial misconduct, Holloway claimed last month he was a "Dead. Man. Walking," within a viral interview clip, which he was reminded about by the supporters of his former club as David Artell's side ran riot, with Aaron Drinan's goal for the hosts a mere consolation after strikes from Andy Cook, Josh Andrews and a brace from Iwan Morgan.

Hollowaytook charge of Grimsby between December 2019 and 2020, winning 13 of his 39 matches in charge, accumulating a 33 percent win ratio in the process.

The experienced figure resigned at Blundell Park following a 2-1 loss to Bradford City which had the club lying 20th in League Two at the time, and the Mariners were eventually relegated back to the National League months later, albeit for a solitary season.

As such, the North Lincolnshire fanbase are not particularly fond of Holloway, who, in return, criticised their actions in his post-match interview.

“On that performance, it is what we deserved, and that hurts me bitterly, because that was a club that I used to work for," he said.

“You wouldn’t think so, would you? The use of language [from their fans] was disgusting, in my opinion, but that is what happens in modern-day football.

“They didn’t say a word when we beat them 4-0 up there two years ago, did they? It is what it is," he added.

Many notable teams have been emotionally exhausting to support in League Two, but which club tops our list at this moment in time?

Discussing the action in League One and League Two from across the weekend, EFL pundits, Tom Garratt and Callum Fowler, were amongst those debating whether Holloway's future in the Swindon job is currently jeopardised.

"Another man who should be under pressure, Ian Holloway. Swindon 1-4 Grimsby," said Garratt on the Pyramid podcast.

"He's just a 'dead man walking', that's what the Grimsby fans were singing to him," Fowler immediately responded.

"Swindon's four-game unbeaten run ends at the hands of Grimsby who have won their last three, scoring 13 goals in that time."

Later discussing Holloway's immediate future, Garratt asked: "is he under pressure?" to which Fowler added: "100%. He's a nutter."

"I'm amazed he's still got a job. Get him gone," the former then stated.

Swindon fans, is Ian Holloway the right man to lead the Robins forward? Comment below!

It remains to be seen where the Robins go from here, with the next opportunity for Holloway to turn the tide being Saturday's trip to Newport County, who sit two places above Swindon on goal difference.

#2100. 5 managers Blackburn Rovers must eye now after devastating Tony Mowbray news

Tony Mowbray has stepped down as Blackburn Rovers head coach due to health issues, and the hunt for his replacement has begun at Ewood Park.

Just a couple of years after stepping down as the head coach of Birmingham City to battle bowel cancer, Mowbray has had to make the unfortunate decision to step down from his role as Blackburn boss due to health issues.

Mowbray issued a heartfelt message to Rovers supporters upon the announcement of his resignation, which can only be described as an incredibly difficult situation for everyone involved, as we at Football League World wish him the best.

Blackburn travel down the road to Burnley next for an enthralling encounter at Turf Moor, with Damien Johnson and Mike Dodds taking charge of first-team duties until a new head coach can be appointed.

As the hunt for a new boss begins, FLW has taken a look at five potential candidates who could realistically assume control at Ewood Park next.

Former Irish forward Damien Duff began his coaching career with Shamrock Rovers in his native country before working as an assistant coach for both Celtic and the Republic of Ireland men's national team.

He took his first steps as a head coach with Shelbourne in 2021, spending three and a half years in charge of the club and guiding them to a first league title in 18 years in 2024.

Duff, who made 220 appearances for Blackburn during his playing career, more than any other club he represented, joined Keith Andrews' coaching staff at Brentford in the summer of 2026, but would surely be tempted by the Blackburn job.

Someone who knows the club inside and out and can seamlessly take the wheel from Mowbray, Duff could be an excellent fit for Blackburn, and simply must be considered.

Austrian coach Gerhard Struber began his career on the touchline in his native country with RB Salzburg, their feeder club, FC Liefering, before joining Wolfsberger and then Barnsley in November 2019.

He would help to keep the Tykes in the Championship in dramatic fashion before leaving Oakwell for the Big Apple with New York Red Bulls just 11 months later, as spells back in Salzburg and then FC Köln preceded his move to Bristol City in the summer of 2025.

After starting bright at Ashton Gate, things quickly derailed, and he was dismissed in March 2026; he has been out of work since, and though likely not a standout candidate for the Blackburn job, he would certainly make for an interesting pick, to say the least.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

A name that will be well-known to supporters of Middlesbrough, Nottingham Forest, and Birmingham City, Aitor Karanka is a fairly left-field suggestion, though his connection with Blackburn's current Head of Football Operations, Rudy Gestede, makes him an interesting candidate.

The Spaniard helped to guide Middlesbrough to the top flight in 2016, as spells with Forest, Birmingham, Granada, and Maccabi Tel Aviv followed, where he now works as a Director of Development for the Spanish national team, taking that role in 2025.

Whether he would be open to a return to management at this stage, especially in the Championship, remains to be seen, but his connection with Gestede during their time together with Boro makes him an interesting candidate.

Rob Edwards is a name that will be linked with almost all Championship jobs that become available until he eventually takes on another role, having been dismissed from his post at Wolverhampton Wanderers unexpectedly over the summer.

The Welshman has spent time in charge of the likes of Forest Green Rovers, Watford, Luton Town, Middlesbrough, and Wolves throughout his coaching career, helping to guide Luton to the Premier League in 2023, which remains his most notable achievement in the technical area at this stage.

Edwards reportedly turned down the opportunity to take the Burnley job over the summer, and should he decide that Ewood Park is his next destination, he will almost immediately have the backing of Rovers supporters for his previous decision to reject the Clarets.

Having taken charge of Luton in November 2022 before guiding them to the top flight, Edwards has the opportunity to do the same with Blackburn, and his name will surely be touted for the position.

What do you make of Mowbray's decision to step down? Who would you like to see in charge of Blackburn following this news? Share your thoughts below!

David Wagner is a name that will put a smile on the faces of many Huddersfield Town supporters, having guided the Terriers to a historic promotion to the Premier League in 2016 before keeping them up against the odds the following campaign.

The German, who was the best man to Jürgen Klopp at the former Liverpool boss' wedding, would spend time with Schalke and Young Boys after Huddersfield before landing at Norwich City in January 2023, helping the Canaries to the Championship play-offs in 2024 before missing out to Leeds United.

He left shortly after the defeat and has been an academy manager at RB Leipzig since 2025, but with an excellent record in the Championship, he would make an interesting candidate and one Blackburn must consider.

#2098. Swansea City face Vitor Matos exit twist - 4 managers must be lined up ASAP

Swansea City fans were rocked by the news on Monday night that Vitor Matos was being looked at by Ligue 1 club Strasbourg, with pressure mounting on current manager Hugo Oliveira.

The Portuguese boss was recently linked with the Crystal Palace post, but after Swansea's form dipped with four defeats in five, and the Eagles appointed Thomas Frank to replace Pierre Sage, Swans supporters felt they'd be able to appreciate Matos a little longer.

Strasbourg's official approach to Matos indicates that the drop in form in the league hasn't impacted his standing amongst managerial circles, and the Ligue 1 side, who are sitting 13th with just three wins from 10, are keen on bringing him in if they pull the trigger on Oliveira.

It's not an ideal scenario for any club to be in, but after a strong start to the Championship campaign, with the South Wales side sitting sixth after 15 games, Swansea will need to act fast to bring someone new in if Matos decides to leave.

These four coaches should be in line to take the reins.

With Swansea's recruitment drive shifting to more of a project-based outcome in the summer, following their partnership with Tony Bloom-led Jamestown Analytics, there could be a more project-heavy hire in the dugout if Matos departs.

However, if the Swans want to keep the momentum from the first 15 games high and hire someone set to continue this promotion push immediately, Carlos Corberan should be right at the top of their list.

The Spaniard has been out of work since leaving Valencia in September. He's helped his boyhood club improve after slow starts and secure a mid-table finish in 2024/25 and 2025/26, and in the Championship, he forged a reputation as one of the better coaches in the division with both Huddersfield Town and West Brom.

Corberan took both of those to the play-offs, despite them being rather worse for wear beforehand, and at the Hawthorns, especially, had his sides playing some of the best football in the division, per the underlying numbers.

There's still plenty of room to improve in this Swansea squad, despite their position, and Corberan could be the missing piece to do so. Plus, the 43-year-old could finally become a Premier League boss in South Wales, something which has eluded him so far in his career.

Perhaps not the most popular choice among the four, but Steve Cooper was the last manager to oversee something close to a successful period at Swansea, finishing in the top six in consecutive seasons between 2019 and 2021.

Since then, the Swans haven't finished any higher than 10th. No parachute payments have helped, but perhaps the numerous changes in managers haven't either.

A look at the 25 biggest clubs in England according to AI chatbot ChatGPT.

Swansea fans weren't totally enamoured with the football being played; others were totally fine with it. What's important, though, is that it brought about results at this level, and for a Swans side who want to maintain this play-off finish, bringing in someone who hasn't finished below the top six at this level wouldn't be a bad decision.

Cooper knows how to come in midway through a campaign and oversee results, too, having taken Nottingham Forest from the bottom of the division to play-off success in the space of eight months.

The task isn't as drastic back in South Wales, so he should be able to achieve some success.

In Matos, Swansea found success with a young, up-and-coming coach from a foreign league. Perhaps they could do so again, but this time with a name they know all too well from his time as a player in South Wales.

What Pablo Hernandez is doing in his first managerial role with boyhood club Castellón in the Spanish second tier is massively going under the radar.

He took over as interim boss in September last year, oversaw their best finish in nearly 20 years, and now has them second with nine wins in 13 games. They've conceded just five goals, which is quite remarkable, and they could be on their way to La Liga for the first time since the 1990/91 campaign.

The 41-year-old already has that connection with Swansea, spending two seasons there as a player and helping them win the EFL Cup in 2013, so he knows what it's like for the South Wales side to be in the top flight and competing regularly.

It may be difficult to persuade him to leave this project at his boyhood club, but the possibility of helping bring a team back to the Premier League could be tempting enough.

Another project-based manager could see Jack Wilshere make the step-up into the Championship after a year cutting his teeth in League One with Luton Town.

The 34-year-old was unable to help the Hatters earn promotion in his first half-season in charge, but his side did show signs of excellence in the latter stages once things started to properly click. Luton went 12 games unbeaten to end the year, winning the EFL Trophy along the way.

Who do you think should replace Vitor Matos if he leaves for Strasbourg, Swans fans? Let us know in the comments.

Now, this season, Wilshere has his side playing good football; the underlying numbers have them among the best teams in the division, and they currently sit fourth, five points off Stockport County in second and with a game in hand.

Swansea haven't shied away from hiring out of the third tier. They did so with Russell Martin in the summer of 2021, and that came after he'd overseen mid-table finishes at MK Dons.

Wilshere is in a much better position as a manager now, and is one of the more promising young coaches in the EFL. He has Championship potential, and Swansea could be the side to harness that.

#2097. Don Goodman delivers brutal Bolton Wanderers warning

Bolton Wanderers have had a rollercoaster first third of the campaign upon their return to the Championship, and though the Trotters have impressed at the top end of the pitch, a lot has been left to desire at the back.

Bolton, following their return to the Championship for the first time since 2019, endured an incredibly busy summer transfer window orchestrated by head coach Steven Schumacher, with as many as 13 new arrivals heading to the Toughsheet Community Stadium.

Among those was an almost entirely new forward line, as the likes of Jayden Nelson, Ryan Hardie, Thelonius Bair, Kylianne Dong, and Samuel Iling-Junior joined the club.

Bolton have hit 20 goals in 15 league matches so far - a steady record. However, with 29 shipped in the other direction, improvements must be made at the back, as an EFL expert has shared their thoughts on the situation.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

Former West Bromwich Albion, Sunderland, and Wolverhampton Wanderers striker Don Goodman has delved into punditry since his retirement from professional football, and has garnered a reputation as an expert on everything in the EFL.

The 60-year-old is not afraid to say exactly what he thinks about the ongoing situations at clubs across the 72, and has shared his thoughts and concerns right now at Bolton, saying: "If you go to watch Bolton Wanderers, you know you're in for a terrific match.

They have been all season. They're a great watch.

"I suspect Steven Schumacher wants them to be entertaining, but here's the issue. They are conceding way too many goals. That will prove costly over a 46-game season.

"They've conceded 29 goals in 15 games. That's almost two goals a game. It means you have to score three goals to win a match. That might happen occasionally, like when they won 4–3 away at Norwich, but it's rare you can rely on scoring three every week.

"They have to become harder to score against. If they don't, I fear they could end up straight back in League One. They're great fun to watch, but they must tighten up defensively."

Only the bottom three of Preston North End, Burnley, and Derby County have shipped more goals than Bolton at this stage of the season, as it has become crystal clear exactly where Steven Schumacher's side needs to address if they are to be successful this year.

Despite this, only Stoke City, Sheffield United, Birmingham City and Swansea City have kept more clean sheets than the Trotters' four after 15 games, as when Bolton concede, it tends to come in waves.

They have conceded three or more goals in seven games this season, as Schumacher simply must find a way to keep the scoreline to a minimum if his side is to pick up enough points for survival come the end of the campaign.

A seven-point buffer separates them from the relegation zone right now, but it could take just a couple of weeks for that to change, as Bolton cannot afford to continue to ship goals at the rate they have been.

What have you made of Bolton Wanderers this season? Is Don Goodman's assessment of the Trotters correct? Share your thoughts below!

Schumacher has worked his way up the league to establish himself as one of the best young coaches in the EFL, having earned promotion to the Championship with Plymouth Argyle and Bolton, whilst keeping Stoke afloat in the second tier against the odds.

Now, he must try and establish the Trotters back in the second tier after seven years away, and whilst on track to do just that as things stand, if the defensive record doesn't improve sooner rather than later, he runs the risk of slipping down the table.

Goodman's warning must be taken into account by Schumacher, who should use the recent international break as an opportunity to do just that.

#2096. CorSport: Fontanella-Khan up and running within Milan’s management – his background and role

James Fontanella-Khan is up and running with his new position at AC Milan, following an interesting background which includes the former Rossoneri man Alberto Gilardino.

It was in the middle of September that various outlets reported that Fontanella-Khan was joining Milan’s management as the new Chief Corporate Affairs Officer. Two months have passed since and the 43-year-old is now up and running with his new gig.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

He has been spotted among the top brass in the stands of San Siro during the most recent games and he was also present at the dinner Gerry Cardinale hosted before the derby. In other words, he’s not exactly working remotely, instead expected to spend most of his time in Milan.

Today’s edition of Corriere dello Sport (via MilanNews) has taken a closer look at Fontanella-Khan, who has various valuable experiences to rely on in his new gig. Most recently, he was the US Financial Editor for the Financial Times, even moderating some conferences that Cardinale spoke at.

Before making waves in the US, however, he actually grew up in Italy and even played football himself. He was a goalkeeper in Piacenza’s youth team, reaching the Primavera of the club, and he was even a teammate of Alberto Gilardino (the former Milan striker) at one point.

After deciding that football wasn’t his path in life, he left Italy to start an internship for the Financial Times in London. He worked his way up and remained at the newspaper for over 20 years before accepting the Milan gig.

As for his role with Milan, according to Corriere dello Sport, he could be described as a General Manager. He handles communications, politics (he will be present at the League assemblies), and represents the club at various events.

Part of his goal is to connect the US mentality of the Rossoneri ownership with the Italian identity of the club, partly relying on his background as a footballer and Milan fan.
